crying before I can even drive home.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to sketch murals, cook for my friends, and play soccer on weekends, but now all of that feels pointless and I usually just ignore messages and stay in my apartment.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I barely get hungry anymore, so a lot of days I just drink coffee at work and maybe eat vending-machine crackers, and my clothes have gotten loose.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: People tell me I move like I’m underwater, and then other times I can’t sit still and keep pacing my apartment or rubbing my hands until they’re sore.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I can read the same page of a patient note or a bill three times and still not know what it says, and even picking what to buy at the store can freeze me up.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: After bad nights I keep thinking it would be easier if I didn’t wake up, and sometimes the thought comes that I should just end it, even though I get scared and tell someone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When the depression is at its worst, I’m also most convinced the supervisors are watching me through the cameras, and my speech gets so tangled that I can’t explain myself clearly.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of a few weeks when I wasn’t in that deep depressed place, but I still believed the cameras and supervisors were monitoring me and I kept acting guarded at work.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve needed coworkers to double-check my rounds and charting, and at home I’ve fallen behind on bills, ignored friends, and let my apartment get out of control. It got bad enough that employee health sent me for an emergency evaluation after I admitted I was thinking about suicide.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 1 year and 5 months, starting after my job switched from steady day shifts to rotating nights and overtime.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The beliefs about being watched and the disorganized behavior have been happening for about 1 year and 1 month.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The depression and loss of interest have been with me for about 1 year and 2 months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / orderly_or_psychiatric_aide / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S021\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you currently believe that someone is trying to harm you or secretly monitor you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t think it’s like some stranger on the street, but at work, yes. I’m pretty sure the supervisors changed my shifts on purpose, and they watch me through the unit cameras to see if I’m unsafe or doing something wrong. People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, everyone’s schedule is messed up, but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt’s like when you’re stocking rooms and you know the cameras are there, and then somebody suddenly shows up right after you do something, like they were waiting. And the schedule thing—overnights, switching days—it’s too lined up. Yeah… yeah, I have. I used to care about stuff a lot more. Like I used to sketch these mural ideas, not for money or anything, just… I’d fill pages with them. And I used to cook for my friends sometimes, like big messy dinners where the kitchen would be completely trashed after, but it felt good.

And soccer on weekends too. I used to actually look forward to that. I had cleats by the door and everything, which is stupid to mention, but now they’re just sitting there and I don’t even know if they still fit right because I haven’t worn them.

Now it all just feels kind of pointless. People text me and I see it, and I’ll think, I should answer, but then I just don’t. I usually stay in my apartment. Sometimes I’ll open my sketchbook and just stare at it, and then I put it away. It’s like the part of me that cared got switched off or something.

Yeah… I think there was. Not the voices part so much, but the being watched thing.

There was this stretch, maybe a few weeks, where I wasn’t, like, in the deep hole exactly. I was still going to work and moving around, and I didn’t feel that crushed feeling every minute. But I was still sure the cameras were on me more than other people, like supervisors were checking what I was doing. I kept watching how I stood, where I looked, what I said.

And at work there’s cameras anyway, so it’s hard to explain, because people act like that makes it normal. Like, every hallway has that feeling, and then someone loses supplies and suddenly everyone’s looking around like it’s a whole investigation… but yeah, even when I wasn’t really depressed, I still felt monitored and I acted guarded. For at least a couple weeks, probably longer.

back, the heavy empty mood and loss of interest have been there for most of this whole period, even during times when the camera worries or scattered talking were quieter.\n- key: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had nights where I barely slept, but then the next day I’m usually dragging, like my body feels heavy and I’m just getting through it.

I don’t think so… not like a clear voice talking to me when nobody’s there.

It’s like I wake up and there’s this weight already there, like I didn’t even get a chance to start the day before it’s sitting on me.\n\nAnd it’s not always like dramatic, I guess, but it’s just… empty. Heavy and empty at the same time, which sounds stupid, but that’s what it feels like. Sometimes after a shift I’ll just sit in my car for a while because I’m crying and I don’t want anyone to see, or I can’t really make myself drive yet. The parking lot lights are weird at night too, they make everything feel kind of far away, and you can hear the carts or doors, but… yeah. Most of the day, most days.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt monitored at work even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There was a few weeks where I wasn’t in that really deep place, like I wasn’t crying in my car every day or feeling completely sunk, but I still felt like they were watching me.\n\nI’d go in and try to act normal, but I was guarded. Like, I’d think about where the cameras were, what the supervisors could see, whether someone was going to come around the corner right after I did something. And even when my mood wasn’t as bad, that feeling didn’t really go away. It was still like I had to be careful at work.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems started, had you had a seizure, head injury, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not that I know of. I never had, like, a big thing happen where everyone was saying it changed me or whatever.\n\nI did bump my head as a kid a couple times, but everybody does, you know? Like running around, gym class, stupid stuff. And at work people talk about injuries all the time, someone twists their back moving a patient and then the whole schedule gets messed up, and then everyone’s mad because we’re short-staffed…\n\nBut no, I don’t think there was a seizure or some serious illness right before all this started. Not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. I used to care about stuff a lot more. Like I used to sketch these mural ideas, not for money or anything, just… I’d fill pages with them. And I used to cook for my friends sometimes, like big messy dinners where the kitchen would be completely trashed after, but it felt good.\n\nAnd soccer on weekends too. I used to actually look forward to that. I had cleats by the door and everything, which is stupid to mention, but now they’re just sitting there and I don’t even know if they still fit right because I haven’t worn them.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. People text me and I see it, and I’ll think, I should answer, but then I just don’t. I usually stay in my apartment. Sometimes I’ll open my sketchbook and just stare at it, and then I put it away. It’s like the part of me that cared got switched off or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something outside of you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think so. Like, I have thoughts I don’t want, sure, but I don’t know if that means someone put them there.\n\nSometimes my mind gets really crowded, like when the hallway at work gets loud and everyone’s calling for different things, and then later I’m still thinking about it even when I’m home. But that’s not exactly someone putting a thought in my head, right?\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed it that way. At least not that I can say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when you were hearing voices or felt sure you were being watched for at least two weeks when you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. Not the voices part so much, but the being watched thing.\n\nThere was this stretch, maybe a few weeks, where I wasn’t, like, in the deep hole exactly. I was still going to work and moving around, and I didn’t feel that crushed feeling every minute. But I was still sure the cameras were on me more than other people, like supervisors were checking what I was doing. I kept watching how I stood, where I looked, what I said.\n\nAnd at work there’s cameras anyway, so it’s hard to explain, because people act like that makes it normal. Like, every hallway has that feeling, and then someone loses supplies and suddenly everyone’s looking around like it’s a whole investigation… but yeah, even when I wasn’t really depressed, I still felt monitored and I acted guarded. For at least a couple weeks, probably longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had nights where I barely slept, but then the next day I’m usually dragging, like my body feels heavy and I’m just getting through it.\n\nThere were times I stayed up late cleaning or just pacing around, but it wasn’t like I felt amazing or full of energy for days. Sometimes work messes with my sleep too, depending on the shift, and then I start thinking about stupid stuff like whether I locked the supply closet or if I left my badge somewhere, and suddenly it’s morning.\n\nBut several days with less sleep and still feeling energized… I don’t think so. Not that I can clearly say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever clearly heard a voice സംസാര to you when no one was actually there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a clear voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I hear my name, but that happens at work too, like someone calls down the hall and then it turns out they were talking to somebody else, or it’s just the carts squeaking or the TV from another room. The building has weird sounds, especially at night, and you start filling in gaps, you know?\n\nBut if you mean like an actual voice having a conversation with me when no one was around… no, I can’t say that’s happened clearly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… I think so. I mean, looking back on it, the low feeling was there for most of the whole thing.

Even when the other stuff would get a little quieter, like when I wasn’t as caught up in it, I still had this heavy, empty kind of mood. And I stopped really caring about things I used to care about. It’s hard to explain because some days I’d still go through the motions, go to work, answer people, do what I had to do… but inside it was like nothing had any weight except feeling bad.

People keep saying it’s just staffing shortages, everyone’s schedule is messed up, but it doesn’t feel random to me.\n\nIt’s like when you’re stocking rooms and you know the cameras are there, and then somebody suddenly shows up right after you do something, like they were waiting. And the schedule thing—overnights, switching days—it’s too lined up. I know how staffing works, I’ve seen the boards, but still… I feel like they’re monitoring me.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually cheerful or high for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it cheerful or high. Like, I can have moments where I’m joking around with somebody at work, or I’m busy and I seem more awake, but that’s not the same thing, right? Sometimes people think if you’re moving fast you’re in a good mood, but half the time you’re just trying to get everything done before someone complains.\n\nSo… unusually cheerful, no, I haven’t really noticed that. Not like walking around feeling amazing or anything. I guess I’m not totally sure what you mean by “high for me,” though.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, honestly. It’s like I wake up and there’s this weight already there, like I didn’t even get a chance to start the day before it’s sitting on me.\n\nAnd it’s not always like dramatic, I guess, but it’s just… empty. Heavy and empty at the same time, which sounds stupid, but that’s what it feels like. Sometimes after a shift I’ll just sit in my car for a while because I’m crying and I don’t want anyone to see, or I can’t really make myself drive yet. The parking lot lights are weird at night too, they make everything feel kind of far away, and you can hear the carts or doors, but… yeah. Most of the day, most days.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt monitored at work even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, that did happen. There was a few weeks where I wasn’t in that really deep place, like I wasn’t crying in my car every day or feeling completely sunk, but I still felt like they were watching me.\n\nI’d go in and try to act normal, but I was guarded. Like, I’d think about where the cameras were, what the supervisors could see, whether someone was going to come around the corner right after I did something. And even when my mood wasn’t as bad, that feeling didn’t really go away. It was still like I had to be careful at work.\n\nDoctor: Before these problems started, had you had a seizure, head injury, or another serious medical illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, not that I know of. I never had, like, a big thing happen where everyone was saying it changed me or whatever.\n\nI did bump my head as a kid a couple times, but everybody does, you know? Like running around, gym class, stupid stuff. And at work people talk about injuries all the time, someone twists their back moving a patient and then the whole schedule gets messed up, and then everyone’s mad because we’re short-staffed…\n\nBut no, I don’t think there was a seizure or some serious illness right before all this started. Not that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself caring much less than usual about things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I have. I used to care about stuff a lot more. Like I used to sketch these mural ideas, not for money or anything, just… I’d fill pages with them. And I used to cook for my friends sometimes, like big messy dinners where the kitchen would be completely trashed after, but it felt good.\n\nAnd soccer on weekends too. I used to actually look forward to that. I had cleats by the door and everything, which is stupid to mention, but now they’re just sitting there and I don’t even know if they still fit right because I haven’t worn them.\n\nNow it all just feels kind of pointless. People text me and I see it, and I’ll think, I should answer, but then I just don’t. I usually stay in my apartment. Sometimes I’ll open my sketchbook and just stare at it, and then I put it away. It’s like the part of me that cared got switched off or something.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if thoughts were being put into your mind by someone or something outside of you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think so. Like, I have thoughts I don’t want, sure, but I don’t know if that means someone put them there.\n\nSometimes my mind gets really crowded, like when the hallway at work gets loud and everyone’s calling for different things, and then later I’m still thinking about it even when I’m home. But that’s not exactly someone putting a thought in my head, right?\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed it that way. At least not that I can say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when you were hearing voices or felt sure you were being watched for at least two weeks when you were not depressed at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think there was. Not the voices part so much, but the being watched thing.\n\nThere was this stretch, maybe a few weeks, where I wasn’t, like, in the deep hole exactly. I was still going to work and moving around, and I didn’t feel that crushed feeling every minute. But I was still sure the cameras were on me more than other people, like supervisors were checking what I was doing. I kept watching how I stood, where I looked, what I said.\n\nAnd at work there’s cameras anyway, so it’s hard to explain, because people act like that makes it normal. Like, every hallway has that feeling, and then someone loses supplies and suddenly everyone’s looking around like it’s a whole investigation… but yeah, even when I wasn’t really depressed, I still felt monitored and I acted guarded. For at least a couple weeks, probably longer.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean, I’ve had nights where I barely slept, but then the next day I’m usually dragging, like my body feels heavy and I’m just getting through it.\n\nThere were times I stayed up late cleaning or just pacing around, but it wasn’t like I felt amazing or full of energy for days. Sometimes work messes with my sleep too, depending on the shift, and then I start thinking about stupid stuff like whether I locked the supply closet or if I left my badge somewhere, and suddenly it’s morning.\n\nBut several days with less sleep and still feeling energized… I don’t think so. Not that I can clearly say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever clearly heard a voice സംസാര to you when no one was actually there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a clear voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I hear my name, but that happens at work too, like someone calls down the hall and then it turns out they were talking to somebody else, or it’s just the carts squeaking or the TV from another room. The building has weird sounds, especially at night, and you start filling in gaps, you know?\n\nBut if you mean like an actual voice having a conversation with me when no one was around… no, I can’t say that’s happened clearly.\n\nDoctor: For at least six months altogether, have you been having these problems—like your thoughts getting hard to organize or feeling watched at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… definitely more than six months. It’s been, I don’t know, about a year and five months now, something like that.\n\nIt started after my job stopped being just regular day shifts. They switched me around to nights and overtime, and everything got kind of… scrambled after that. The schedule was never the same, and I’d be there at weird hours, and it just sort of kept going from there.\n\nSo yeah, not just a short thing. It’s been over a year.\n\nDoctor: During the whole time these problems have been going on, have you been depressed for most of that time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, looking back on it, the low feeling was there for most of the whole thing.\n\nEven when the other stuff would get a little quieter, like when I wasn’t as caught up in it, I still had this heavy, empty kind of mood.
